亨利·塞勒（Henry Saylor）在科温顿与安东尼奥·芬奇（Antonio Finch）的一次争吵中被杀，他生前是辛辛那提《商业报》（Cincinnati *Commercial*）的一名记者。1859年，辛辛那提葡萄街上一栋空置的住宅因据称每晚都能看到的奇怪景象和听到的怪异声响，成为当地轰动的焦点。根据附近许多信誉良好的居民的证词，这些现象只能解释为闹鬼。人行道上的人群看到一些外形极其陌生的身影进进出出。没人能说清它们是从草坪的何处出现并走向前门进入屋内的，也没人能确定它们出来时消失的确切地点；或者说，虽然每位目击者都对这些细节相当肯定，但没有两个人的说法一致。他们对身影本身的描述同样众说纷纭。一些胆子较大的好奇者，有几个晚上冒险站在门阶上试图拦截它们，或者失败的话，就近距离看个仔细。据说，这些勇敢的人无法合力撞开门，总是被某种看不见的力量从台阶上猛摔下来，严重受伤；随后门立刻自行打开，似乎出于自愿，以接纳或释放某个幽灵般的访客。这栋住宅被称为罗斯科宅（Roscoe house），一个同姓的家庭曾在那里住了几年，然后一个个地消失了，最后离开的是一位老妇人。关于谋杀和连环凶案的流言一直甚嚣尘上，但从未得到证实。

===Original===
One day during the prevalence of the excitement Saylor presented himself at the office of the *Commercial* for orders. He received a note from the city editor which read as follows: "Go and pass the night alone in the haunted house in Vine street and if anything occurs worth while make two columns." Saylor obeyed his superior; he could not afford to lose his position on the paper.

===Chinese===
就在骚动最盛的一天，塞勒来到《商业报》办公室等候任务。他从城市版编辑那里收到一张字条，上面写着："去葡萄街的凶宅独自过夜，如果有值得报道的事情发生，写两栏报道。"塞勒服从了上司；他丢不起报社的工作。

===Original===
Apprising the police of his intention, he effected an entrance through a rear window before dark, walked through the deserted rooms, bare of furniture, dusty and desolate, and seating himself at last in the parlor on an old sofa which he had dragged in from another room watched the deepening of the gloom as night came on. Before it was altogether dark the curious crowd had collected in the street, silent, as a rule, and expectant, with here and there a scoffer uttering his incredulity and courage with scornful remarks or ribald cries. None knew of the anxious watcher inside. He feared to make a light; the uncurtained windows would have betrayed his presence, subjecting him to insult, possibly to injury. Moreover, he was too conscientious to do anything to enfeeble his impressions and unwilling to alter any of the customary conditions under which the manifestations were said to occur.

===Chinese===
他将自己的意图告知了警察，在天黑前从一扇后窗进入屋内，穿过空荡荡的房间——没有家具，尘土飞扬，荒凉不堪——最后在客厅里坐在一张他从另一个房间拖进来的旧沙发上，看着夜幕降临后黑暗逐渐加深。天尚未全黑，好奇的人群已聚集在街上，通常都沉默而期待，不时有个怀疑者发出轻蔑的嘲笑或粗俗的叫喊来表达他的怀疑和勇气。没人知道里面那个焦虑的守望者。他不敢点灯；没有窗帘的窗户会暴露他的行踪，使他遭受侮辱，甚至可能受到伤害。此外，他太有责任心，不会做任何削弱自己印象的事，也不愿改变据称那些异象发生时的任何常规条件。

===Original===
It was now dark outside, but light from the street faintly illuminated the part of the room that he was in. He had set open every door in the whole interior, above and below, but all the outer ones were locked and bolted. Sudden exclamations from the crowd caused him to spring to the window and look out. He saw the figure of a man moving rapidly across the lawn toward the building—saw it ascend the steps; then a projection of the wall concealed it. There was a noise as of the opening and closing of the hall door; he heard quick, heavy footsteps along the passage—heard them ascend the stairs—heard them on the uncarpeted floor of the chamber immediately overhead.

===Chinese===
此刻外面已是一片漆黑，但街上的灯光微弱地照亮了他所在的房间部分。他打开了整栋房子内部上下所有的门，但所有外门都上了锁并插上了门闩。人群突然发出的惊呼使他跳到窗前向外望去。他看见一个人影迅速穿过草坪向房子走来——看见它登上台阶；接着墙壁的一个突出部分遮住了它。传来一阵像是前厅门开关的响声；他听到走廊上急促而沉重的脚步声——听到它们登上楼梯——听到它们在头顶正上方那间没有铺地毯的房间地板上走动。

===Original===
Saylor promptly drew his pistol, and groping his way up the stairs entered the chamber, dimly lighted from the street. No one was there. He heard footsteps in an adjoining room and entered that. It was dark and silent. He struck his foot against some object on the floor, knelt by it, passed his hand over it. It was a human head—that of a woman. Lifting it by the hair this iron-nerved man returned to the half-lighted room below, carried it near the window and attentively examined it. While so engaged he was half conscious of the rapid opening and closing of the outer door, of footfalls sounding all about him. He raised his eyes from the ghastly object of his attention and saw himself the center of a crowd of men and women dimly seen; the room was thronged with them. He thought the people had broken in.

===Chinese===
塞勒立刻拔出手枪，摸索着走上楼梯，进入那间被街灯微弱照亮的房间。里面没人。他听到隔壁房间有脚步声，便走了进去。里面一片漆黑，寂静无声。他的脚踢到了地板上的某个物体，跪下来，用手摸索它。那是一个人头——一个女人的。这个神经坚韧的男人拎起头发，将它带回楼下半明半暗的房间，拿到窗边仔细检查。当他忙于此事时，半意识地感觉到外门在快速开关，脚步声在他周围响起。他从手中那恐怖的物体上抬起眼睛，看到自己成了一群隐约可见的男女的中心；房间里挤满了人。他以为这些人是破门而入的。

===Original===
"Ladies and gentlemen," he said, coolly, "you see me under suspicious circumstances, but"—his voice was drowned in peals of laughter—such laughter as is heard in asylums for the insane. The persons about him pointed at the object in his hand and their merriment increased as he dropped it and it went rolling among their feet. They danced about it with gestures grotesque and attitudes obscene and indescribable. They struck it with their feet, urging it about the room from wall to wall; pushed and overthrew one another in their struggles to kick it; cursed and screamed and sang snatches of ribald songs as the battered head bounced about the room as if in terror and trying to escape. At last it shot out of the door into the hall, followed by all, with tumultuous haste. That moment the door closed with a sharp concussion. Saylor was alone, in dead silence.

===Chinese===
"女士们，先生们，"他镇定地说，"你们看到我身处可疑的境地，但是"——他的声音被一阵阵大笑淹没——那种笑声仿佛出自疯人院。围着他的人指着那件东西，当他松手让它滚落在他们脚边时，他们的欢闹更甚。他们围着它跳舞，做出怪异而淫秽、难以形容的姿势。他们用脚踢它，在房间里从这面墙赶到那面墙；为了踢它而互相推挤、撞倒对方；当那颗被打烂的头颅在房间里弹来弹去，仿佛惊恐万状试图逃脱时，他们咒骂、尖叫，唱着粗俗歌曲的片段。最后，它猛地射出门外冲进走廊，所有人紧随其后，一片喧嚣匆忙。就在那一刻，门砰地一声关上了。塞勒独自一人，死寂一片。

===Original===
Carefully putting away his pistol, which all the time he had held in his hand, he went to a window and looked out. The street was deserted and silent; the lamps were extinguished; the roofs and chimneys of the houses were sharply outlined against the dawn-light in the east. He left the house, the door yielding easily to his hand, and walked to the *Commercial* office. The city editor was still in his office—asleep. Saylor waked him and said: "I have been at the haunted house."

===Chinese===
他小心地收好一直握在手里的手枪，走到一扇窗前向外望去。街上空无一人，寂静无声；路灯已熄灭；房屋的屋顶和烟囱在东方曙光的映衬下轮廓分明。他离开了房子，门轻易地在他手下开启，步行前往《商业报》办公室。城市版编辑还在办公室里——睡着了。塞勒叫醒他说："我去过那栋凶宅了。"

===Original===
The editor stared blankly as if not wholly awake. "Good God!" he cried, "are you Saylor?"

===Chinese===
编辑茫然地瞪着他，仿佛还没完全清醒。"天哪！"他叫道，"你是塞勒？"

===Original===
"Yes—why not?" The editor made no answer, but continued staring.

===Chinese===
"是的——怎么不是？"编辑没有回答，继续瞪着。

===Original===
"I passed the night there—it seems," said Saylor.

===Chinese===
"我在那里过了一夜——看起来是这样，"塞勒说。

===Original===
"They say that things were uncommonly quiet out there," the editor said, trifling with a paperweight upon which he had dropped his eyes, "did anything occur?"

===Chinese===
"他们说那边异常安静，"编辑说，把玩着一个他垂眼盯着的镇纸，"发生什么事了吗？"

===Original===
"Nothing whatever."

===Chinese===
"什么也没发生。"

## A Vine on a House / 房子上的藤蔓

===Original===
About three miles from the little town of Norton, in Missouri, on the road leading to Maysville, stands an old house that was last occupied by a family named Harding. Since 1886 no one has lived in it, nor is anyone likely to live in it again. Time and the disfavor of persons dwelling thereabout are converting it into a rather picturesque ruin. An observer unacquainted with its history would hardly put it into the category of "haunted houses," yet in all the region round such is its evil reputation. Its windows are without glass, its doorways without doors; there are wide breaches in the shingle roof, and for lack of paint the weatherboarding is a dun gray. But these unfailing signs of the supernatural are partly concealed and greatly softened by the abundant foliage of a large vine overrunning the entire structure. This vine—of a species which no botanist has ever been able to name—has an important part in the story of the house.

===Chinese===
在密苏里州诺顿小镇（Norton）通往梅斯维尔（Maysville）的公路旁，大约三英里处，矗立着一座老房子，最后一个住在那里的是哈丁（Harding）一家。自1886年起，再没有人住在里面，以后大概也不会有人住了。岁月的侵蚀加上附近居民的嫌恶正在把它变成一座颇有几分破败美感的废墟。不了解其历史的观察者几乎不会把它归入"凶宅"一类，但在周围整个地区，它确实背负着如此恶名。窗户没有玻璃，门洞没有门扇；木瓦屋顶上豁开了大口子，护墙板因常年失漆而呈暗灰色。但这些超自然现象的典型标志被一棵覆盖了整栋建筑的大藤蔓的茂密枝叶部分遮挡、大大柔化了。这棵藤蔓——没有任何植物学家能确定其属种——在关于这座房子的故事中扮演着重要角色。

===Original===
The Harding family consisted of Robert Harding, his wife Matilda, Miss Julia Went, who was her sister, and two young children. Robert Harding was a silent, cold-mannered man who made no friends in the neighborhood and apparently cared to make none. He was about forty years old, frugal and industrious, and made a living from the little farm which is now overgrown with brush and brambles. He and his sister-in-law were rather tabooed by their neighbors, who seemed to think that they were seen too frequently together—not entirely their fault, for at these times they evidently did not challenge observation. The moral code of rural Missouri is stern and exacting.

===Chinese===
哈丁一家包括罗伯特·哈丁（Robert Harding）、他的妻子玛蒂尔达、妻子的妹妹朱莉娅·温特小姐（Miss Julia Went），以及两个年幼的孩子。罗伯特·哈丁是个沉默寡言、性情冷漠的人，在邻居中没有交朋友，看起来也不想交朋友。他大约四十岁，节俭勤勉，靠经营一小片农场维持生计——如今那片地已长满了灌木和荆棘。他和妻妹在邻居中相当受排斥，似乎人们觉得他们在一起的时间太多了——这倒不完全是他们的错，因为他们在那种时候显然并不在意旁人的目光。密苏里乡间的道德准则严厉而苛刻。

===Original===
Mrs. Harding was a gentle, sad-eyed woman, lacking a left foot.

===Chinese===
哈丁太太是个温柔而忧伤的女人，缺了一只左脚。

===Original===
At some time in 1884 it became known that she had gone to visit her mother in Iowa. That was what her husband said in reply to inquiries, and his manner of saying it did not encourage further questioning. She never came back, and two years later, without selling his farm or anything that was his, or appointing an agent to look after his interests, or removing his household goods, Harding, with the rest of the family, left the country. Nobody knew whither he went; nobody at that time cared. Naturally, whatever was movable about the place soon disappeared and the deserted house became "haunted" in the manner of its kind.

===Chinese===
1884年的某个时候，人们得知她去了爱荷华州探望母亲。这是她丈夫对询问的答复，而他说话的口气不鼓励进一步追问。她再也没有回来，两年后，哈丁没有卖掉农场或任何属于他的东西，也没有指定代理人照管他的财产，更没有搬走家什，便带着其余家人离开了此地。没人知道他去了哪里；那时候也没人在乎。自然，这地方一切能搬走的东西很快就没了，这座废弃的房子也像同类房屋一样变得"闹鬼"了。

===Original===
One summer evening, four or five years later, the Rev. J. Gruber, of Norton, and a Maysville attorney named Hyatt met on horseback in front of the Harding place. Having business matters to discuss, they hitched their animals and going to the house sat on the porch to talk. Some humorous reference to the somber reputation of the place was made and forgotten as soon as uttered, and they talked of their business affairs until it grew almost dark. The evening was oppressively warm, the air stagnant.

===Chinese===
四五年后的一个夏夜，诺顿的J.格鲁伯牧师（Rev. J. Gruber）和梅斯维尔一位名叫海厄特（Hyatt）的律师骑马在哈丁家门前相遇。他们有业务要谈，便把马拴好，走到房子的门廊上坐下聊天。有人幽默地提到了这地方阴森的名声，话一出口就忘了，他们谈着公事，直到天色几乎全黑。傍晚闷热难当，空气凝滞。

===Original===
Presently both men started from their seats in surprise: a long vine that covered half the front of the house and dangled its branches from the edge of the porch above them was visibly and audibly agitated, shaking violently in every stem and leaf.

===Chinese===
不一会儿，两个人都惊讶地从座位上跳了起来：一棵长长的藤蔓覆盖了房子正面的一半，枝条从他们头顶的门廊边缘垂下来，此刻正在清晰可见、清晰可闻地剧烈摇晃，每一根茎和每一片叶子都在颤抖。

===Original===
"We shall have a storm," Hyatt exclaimed.

===Chinese===
"暴风雨要来了，"海厄特叫道。

===Original===
Gruber said nothing, but silently directed the other's attention to the foliage of adjacent trees, which showed no movement; even the delicate tips of the boughs silhouetted against the clear sky were motionless. They hastily passed down the steps to what had been a lawn and looked upward at the vine, whose entire length was now visible. It continued in violent agitation, yet they could discern no disturbing cause.

===Chinese===
格鲁伯没有说话，只是默默地指给对方看附近树木的枝叶——它们纹丝不动；就连映衬在晴朗天空下的纤细枝梢也静止不动。他们匆匆走下台阶，来到曾经是草坪的地方，抬头望向藤蔓，此刻它的全部长度都清晰可见。它依然在剧烈地摇曳着，但他们找不到任何使其动荡的原因。

===Original===
"Let us leave," said the minister.

===Chinese===
"我们走吧，"牧师说。

===Original===
And leave they did. Forgetting that they had been traveling in opposite directions, they rode away together. They went to Norton, where they related their strange experience to several discreet friends. The next evening, at about the same hour, accompanied by two others whose names are not recalled, they were again on the porch of the Harding house, and again the mysterious phenomenon occurred: the vine was violently agitated while under the closest scrutiny from root to tip, nor did their combined strength applied to the trunk serve to still it. After an hour's observation they retreated, no less wise, it is thought, than when they had came.

===Chinese===
他们果然走了。他们忘了来时走的是相反的方向，一起骑马离开了。他们去了诺顿，向几位谨慎的朋友讲述了这段奇异的经历。第二天傍晚，大约同一时间，另外还有两个名字已不可考的人陪同，他们再次来到哈丁家的门廊上，而神秘的现象又一次发生了：在他们从根到梢的最密切监视下，藤蔓剧烈地摇摆着，他们合力扳住树干也无法使其静止。观察了一个小时后，他们撤退了——据说并不比来时更明白多少。

===Original===
No great time was required for these singular facts to rouse the curiosity of the entire neighborhood. By day and by night crowds of persons assembled at the Harding house "seeking a sign." It does not appear that any found it, yet so credible were the witnesses mentioned that none doubted the reality of the "manifestations" to which they testified.

===Chinese===
这些离奇的事实没过多久就激起了整个社区的好奇心。白天黑夜都有成群的人聚集在哈丁家门前"寻找征兆"。看起来没有人找到了，但上述证人的可信度是如此之高，以至于没有人怀疑他们所证实的那些"异象"的真实性。

===Original===
By either a happy inspiration or some destructive design, it was one day proposed—nobody appeared to know from whom the suggestion came—to dig up the vine, and after a good deal of debate this was done. Nothing was found but the root, yet nothing could have been more strange!

===Chinese===
不知是出于一个绝妙的灵感还是某种破坏性的目的，有一天有人提议——没人知道是谁提的——把藤蔓挖出来，经过一番激烈争论，人们照做了。除了根什么也没找到，但没有什么比这更离奇的了！

===Original===
For five or six feet from the trunk, which had at the surface of the ground a diameter of several inches, it ran downward, single and straight, into a loose, friable earth; then it divided and subdivided into rootlets, fibers and filaments, most curiously interwoven. When carefully freed from soil they showed a singular formation. In their ramifications and doublings back upon themselves they made a compact network, having in size and shape an amazing resemblance to the human figure. Head, trunk and limbs were there; even the fingers and toes were distinctly defined; and many professed to see in the distribution and arrangement of the fibers in the globular mass representing the head a grotesque suggestion of a face. The figure was horizontal; the smaller roots had begun to unite at the breast.

===Chinese===
从地面上直径有好几英寸的主干向下延伸五六英尺，笔直地扎入松散易碎的泥土中；然后分叉、再分叉，变成根须、纤维和细丝，极其精巧地交织在一起。当仔细清除掉泥土后，它们呈现出一种奇异的形态。在盘曲交错、回环往复中，它们编织成一个致密的网络，在大小和形状上惊人地酷似一个人体。头、躯干和四肢一应俱全；甚至连手指和脚趾都清晰可辨；许多人声称，在代表头部的那个球状体中，纤维的分布和排列构成了一张怪诞的面孔。人形是横卧的；较细的根须已经在胸部位置开始交汇。

===Original===
In point of resemblance to the human form this image was imperfect. At about ten inches from one of the knees, the cilia forming that leg had abruptly doubled backward and inward upon their course of growth. The figure lacked the left foot.

===Chinese===
就与人形的相似度而言，这个形象并不完美。在距离一个膝盖大约十英寸处，构成那条腿的纤维须突然折回，朝内侧弯折。这个形象缺少了左脚。

===Original===
There was but one inference—the obvious one; but in the ensuing excitement as many courses of action were proposed as there were incapable counselors. The matter was settled by the sheriff of the county, who as the lawful custodian of the abandoned estate ordered the root replaced and the excavation filled with the earth that had been removed.

===Chinese===
只有一种推论——那显而易见的一种；但在随之而来的兴奋中，各人提出的行动方案之多不亚于无能的顾问。此事最终由县治安官裁定，他作为被弃地产的法定管理人下令把根放回去，并用挖出来的土把坑填平。

===Original===
Later inquiry brought out only one fact of relevancy and significance: Mrs. Harding had never visited her relatives in Iowa, nor did they know that she was supposed to have done so.

===Chinese===
后来的调查只弄清了一个相关且重要的事实：哈丁太太从未去过爱荷华州探望她的亲戚，她的亲戚也不知道有这回事。

===Original===
Of Robert Harding and the rest of his family nothing is known. The house retains its evil reputation, but the replanted vine is as orderly and well-behaved a vegetable as a nervous person could wish to sit under of a pleasant night, when the katydids grate out their immemorial revelation and the distant whippoorwill signifies his notion of what ought to be done about it.

===Chinese===
关于罗伯特·哈丁及其余家人，一无所知。那座房子依然背负着恶名，但重新种下的藤蔓却是最安分守己的植物，在宁静的夜晚，一个神经质的人完全可以放心地坐在它下面——那时螽斯（katydids）正嘎吱嘎吱地吟唱着亘古不变的秘密，远处的夜鹰（whippoorwill）则表达着它对这一切应当如何了结的看法。

### At Old Man Eckert’s / 在老埃克特家

===Original===
Philip Eckert lived for many years in an old, weather-stained wooden house about three miles from the little town of Marion, in Vermont. There must be quite a number of persons living who remember him, not unkindly, I trust, and know something of the story that I am about to tell.

===Chinese===
菲利普·埃克特（Philip Eckert）在佛蒙特州马里恩镇附近约三英里处一栋饱经风霜的旧木屋里住了许多年。现在应该还有不少人记得他，但愿他们对他并无恶意，并且知道一些我即将讲述的故事。

===Original===
"Old Man Eckert," as he was always called, was not of a sociable disposition and lived alone. As he was never known to speak of his own affairs nobody thereabout knew anything of his past, nor of his relatives if he had any. Without being particularly ungracious or repellent in manner or speech, he managed somehow to be immune to impertinent curiosity, yet exempt from the evil repute with which it commonly revenges itself when baffled; so far as I know, Mr. Eckert's renown as a reformed assassin or a retired pirate of the Spanish Main had not reached any ear in Marion. He got his living cultivating a small and not very fertile farm.

===Chinese===
“老埃克特”（Old Man Eckert），正如人们总这样称呼他，性情并不合群，独自生活。由于从未有人听他谈论过自己的事，附近没人知道他的过去，也不知道他是否有亲戚。尽管他的举止言谈并不特别粗鲁或令人反感，他不知怎么就能免疫于无礼的好奇心，又不会招致好奇受挫时常有的恶名缠身；据我所知，埃克特先生作为改过自新的刺客或西属美洲海域（Spanish Main）退休海盗的名声，尚未传入马里恩镇任何人的耳朵。他靠耕种一块不大且不太肥沃的农场为生。

===Original===
One day he disappeared and a prolonged search by his neighbors failed to turn him up or throw any light upon his whereabouts or whyabouts. Nothing indicated preparation to leave: all was as he might have left it to go to the spring for a bucket of water. For a few weeks little else was talked of in that region; then "old man Eckert" became a village tale for the ear of the stranger. I do not know what was done regarding his property—the correct legal thing, doubtless. The house was standing, still vacant and conspicuously unfit, when I last heard of it, some twenty years afterward.

===Chinese===
一天他失踪了，邻居们进行了长时间的搜寻，但未能找到他，也无法说明他的行踪或去向。没有任何迹象表明他准备离开：一切就像是他去泉边打一桶水时离开的样子。几周内，那个地区几乎只谈论这件事；接着“老埃克特”成了专讲给外乡人听的村庄传说。我不知道他的财产如何处理了——毫无疑问是合法的。在我最后一次听说那栋房子时，大约二十年后，它依然矗立着，空置且明显不宜居住。

===Original===
Of course it came to be considered "haunted," and the customary tales were told of moving lights, dolorous sounds and startling apparitions. At one time, about five years after the disappearance, these stories of the supernatural became so rife, or through some attesting circumstances seemed so important, that some of Marion's most serious citizens deemed it well to investigate, and to that end arranged for a night session on the premises. The parties to this undertaking were John Holcomb, an apothecary; Wilson Merle, a lawyer, and Andrus C. Palmer, the teacher of the public school, all men of consequence and repute. They were to meet at Holcomb's house at eight o'clock in the evening of the appointed day and go together to the scene of their vigil, where certain arrangements for their comfort, a provision of fuel and the like, for the season was winter, had been already made.

===Chinese===
当然，它开始被认为是“闹鬼的”，并且流传着关于移动的光影、悲戚的声响和骇人幽灵的常规故事。大约在失踪五年后的一个时期，这些关于超自然现象的故事变得如此流行，或者由于某些确凿的迹象显得如此重要，以至于马里恩镇一些最严肃的公民认为最好调查一下，为此安排了在原地过夜的活动。参与此事的人是药剂师约翰·霍尔库姆（John Holcomb）；律师威尔逊·默尔（Wilson Merle），以及公立学校的教师安德鲁斯·C·帕尔默（Andrus C. Palmer），他们都是举足轻重、声誉卓著的人物。他们约定在指定日期的晚上八点在霍尔库姆家会合，然后一同前往守夜地点，那里已经为他们准备了一些舒适的设施、燃料等，因为当时是冬季。

===Original===
Palmer did not keep the engagement, and after waiting a half-hour for him the others went to the Eckert house without him. They established themselves in the principal room, before a glowing fire, and without other light than it gave, awaited events. It had been agreed to speak as little as possible: they did not even renew the exchange of views regarding the defection of Palmer, which had occupied their minds on the way.

===Chinese===
帕尔默没有赴约，在等了他半个小时后，其他人没等他就去了埃克特家。他们在主房间里，在熊熊燃烧的炉火前安顿下来，除了炉火的光亮外没有其他照明，等待着事情发生。他们事先约定尽量少说话：他们甚至没有重新讨论帕尔默的缺席，尽管路上他们一直在想这件事。

===Original===
Probably an hour had passed without incident when they heard (not without emotion, doubtless) the sound of an opening door in the rear of the house, followed by footfalls in the room adjoining that in which they sat. The watchers rose to their feet, but stood firm, prepared for whatever might ensue. A long silence followed—how long neither would afterward undertake to say. Then the door between the two rooms opened and a man entered.

===Chinese===
大概一个小时过去了，没有发生什么事，这时他们听到（无疑带着情绪）房子后面一扇门打开的声音，紧接着是隔壁房间里的脚步声。守夜人站了起来，但站得稳稳的，准备应对可能发生的事情。接着是一阵长久的沉默——有多久，事后他们谁也不敢说。然后两个房间之间的门开了，一个男人走了进来。

===Original===
It was Palmer. He was pale, as if from excitement—as pale as the others felt themselves to be. His manner, too, was singularly distrait: he neither responded to their salutations nor so much as looked at them, but walked slowly across the room in the light of the failing fire and opening the front door passed out into the darkness.

===Chinese===
是帕尔默。他脸色苍白，像是因激动所致——和其他人觉得自己的一样苍白。而且他的举止异常恍惚：他既没有回应他们的问候，甚至没有看他们一眼，只是借着将熄的炉火光芒慢慢地穿过房间，打开前门，走进了黑暗中。

===Original===
It seems to have been the first thought of both men that Palmer was suffering from fright—that something seen, heard or imagined in the back room had deprived him of his senses. Acting on the same friendly impulse both ran after him through the open door. But neither they nor anyone ever again saw or heard of Andrus Palmer!

===Chinese===
两人最初似乎都想到帕尔默是受了惊吓——在后房间里看到、听到或想象到的什么东西让他失去了理智。出于同样的友好冲动，两人都跟着他跑出了敞开的门。但他们或其他人再也没有见到或听说过安德鲁斯·帕尔默！

===Original===
This much was ascertained the next morning. During the session of Messrs. Holcomb and Merle at the "haunted house" a new snow had fallen to a depth of several inches upon the old. In this snow Palmer's trail from his lodging in the village to the back door of the Eckert house was conspicuous. But there it ended: from the front door nothing led away but the tracks of the two men who swore that he preceded them. Palmer's disappearance was as complete as that of "old man Eckert" himself—whom, indeed, the editor of the local paper somewhat graphically accused of having "reached out and pulled him in."

===Chinese===
第二天早上查明了这些情况。在霍尔库姆和默尔两位先生于“闹鬼房子”逗留期间，新雪在旧雪上积了几英寸深。在这片雪地上，帕尔默从村里住处到埃克特家后门的足迹清晰可见。但足迹到此为止：前门外除了那两个发誓说他走在他们前面的男人的脚印外，没有任何痕迹。帕尔默的失踪和“老埃克特”本人一样彻底——当地报纸的编辑甚至有些形象地指责说他“伸出手把他拉了进去”。

### The Spook House / 幽灵宅

===Original===
On the road leading north from Manchester, in eastern Kentucky, to Booneville, twenty miles away, stood, in 1862, a wooden plantation house of a somewhat better quality than most of the dwellings in that region. The house was destroyed by fire in the year following﻿—probably by some stragglers from the retreating column of General George W. Morgan, when he was driven from Cumberland Gap to the Ohio river by General Kirby Smith. At the time of its destruction, it had for four or five years been vacant. The fields about it were overgrown with brambles, the fences gone, even the few negro quarters, and outhouses generally, fallen partly into ruin by neglect and pillage; for the negroes and poor whites of the vicinity found in the building and fences an abundant supply of fuel, of which they availed themselves without hesitation, openly and by daylight. By daylight alone; after nightfall no human being except passing strangers ever went near the place.
It was known as the "Spook House." That it was tenanted by evil spirits, visible, audible and active, no one in all that region doubted any more than he doubted what he was told of Sundays by the traveling preacher. Its owner's opinion of the matter was unknown; he and his family had disappeared one night and no trace of them had ever been found. They left everything﻿—household goods, clothing, provisions, the horses in the stable, the cows in the field, the negroes in the quarters﻿—all as it stood; nothing was missing﻿—except a man, a woman, three girls, a boy and a babe! It was not altogether surprising that a plantation where seven human beings could be simultaneously effaced and nobody the wiser should be under some suspicion.

===Chinese===
1862年，在肯塔基州东部从曼彻斯特通往二十英里外布恩维尔的公路旁，矗立着一座木制种植园宅邸，其质量略优于该地区大多数民居。次年这宅子毁于大火——大概出自乔治·W·摩根（George W. Morgan）将军溃退纵队中某些掉队士兵之手，当时柯比·史密斯（Kirby Smith）将军将摩根从坎伯兰隘口（Cumberland Gap）一路赶到了俄亥俄河。被焚毁时，这宅子已空置了四五年。周围的田野长满了荆棘，篱笆消失不见，连黑人住的棚屋和附属建筑也因疏于管理和掠夺而半数倾颓；因为附近的黑人和贫穷白人视此建筑和篱笆为取之不尽的燃料来源，他们毫不犹豫地公开在白天取用。仅限白天；天黑之后，除了路过的陌生人，再无人靠近此地。
这地方被称为"幽灵宅（Spook House）"。在那一带，没人怀疑它被恶灵占据——那些恶灵可见、可闻且活跃——正如没人怀疑巡回牧师在星期天告诉他们的话。宅主人对此事的看法不得而知；他和他的家人一夜之间消失得无影无踪，从未留下任何痕迹。他们留下了一切——家具、衣物、食品、马厩里的马、田野里的牛、棚屋里的黑人——一切照旧；什么都没少——只少了一个男人、一个女人、三个女孩、一个男孩和一个婴儿！一个七口之家能在一夜之间被同时抹去而无人察觉，这地方因此蒙上些嫌疑，实在不足为奇。

===Original===
One night in June, 1859, two citizens of Frankfort, Col. J. C. McArdle, a lawyer, and Judge Myron Veigh, of the State Militia, were driving from Booneville to Manchester. Their business was so important that they decided to push on, despite the darkness and the mutterings of an approaching storm, which eventually broke upon them just as they arrived opposite the "Spook House." The lightning was so incessant that they easily found their way through the gateway and into a shed, where they hitched and unharnessed their team. They then went to the house, through the rain, and knocked at all the doors without getting any response. Attributing this to the continuous uproar of the thunder they pushed at one of the doors, which yielded. They entered without further ceremony and closed the door. That instant they were in darkness and silence. Not a gleam of the lightning's unceasing blaze penetrated the windows or crevices; not a whisper of the awful tumult without reached them there. It was as if they had suddenly been stricken blind and deaf, and McArdle afterward said that for a moment he believed himself to have been killed by a stroke of lightning as he crossd the threshold. The rest of this adventure can as well be related in his own words, from the Frankfort *Advocate* of August 6, 1876:

===Chinese===
1859年6月的一个夜晚，法兰克福（Frankfort）的两位市民——律师J·C·麦卡德尔上校（Col. J. C. McArdle）和州民兵法官迈伦·维格（Myron Veigh）——正驱车从布恩维尔赶往曼彻斯特。他们的事务如此重要，以至于他们决定不顾黑暗和迫近的风暴低鸣继续赶路，而那风暴最终在他们抵达"幽灵宅"对面时倾泻而下。闪电如此密集，他们轻松穿过大门进入一间棚屋，在那里拴好并卸下了马具。然后他们穿过雨幕走向宅子，敲遍了所有门窗，没有得到任何回应。他们将此归因于雷声的持续轰鸣，于是推了推其中一扇门，门应手而开。他们未再多礼便走了进去，随手关上了门。刹那间，他们陷入了黑暗与死寂。闪电不间断的光芒未能穿透窗户或缝隙；外面可怖的喧嚣连一丝也未能传入。仿佛他们突然失去了视觉和听觉。麦卡德尔后来回忆说，有那么一刻他以为自己在跨过门槛时被一道闪电劈死。这段冒险的余下部分最好用他本人的话来讲述，摘自1876年8月6日法兰克福《倡导者报》（*Advocate*）：

===Original===
"When I had somewhat recovered from the dazing effect of the transition from uproar to silence, my first impulse was to reopen the door which I had closed, and from the knob of which I was not conscious of having removed my hand; I felt it distinctly, still in the clasp of my fingers. My notion was to ascertain by stepping again into the storm whether I had been deprived of sight and hearing. I turned the doorknob and pulled open the door. It led into another room!
"This apartment was suffused with a faint greenish light, the source of which I could not determine, making everything distinctly visible, though nothing was sharply defined. Everything, I say, but in truth the only objects within the blank stone walls of that room were human corpses. In number they were perhaps eight or ten﻿—it may well be understood that I did not truly count them. They were of different ages, or rather sizes, from infancy up, and of both sexes. All were prostrate on the floor, excepting one, apparently a young woman, who sat up, her back supported by an angle of the wall. A babe was clasped in the arms of another and older woman. A half-grown lad lay face downward across the legs of a full-bearded man. One or two were nearly naked, and the hand of a young girl held the fragment of a gown which she had torn open at the breast. The bodies were in various stages of decay, all greatly shrunken in face and figure. Some were but little more than skeletons.

===Chinese===
"当我稍微从喧嚣转为寂静所带来的眩晕中恢复过来时，我第一个冲动是重新打开那扇我刚关上的门，我发觉自己的手并未从门把上移开——我能清楚地感到它仍握在我指间。我的想法是重新踏入暴风雨中，以确认自己是否丧失了视觉和听觉。我转了转门把手，拉开门。它通向另一个房间！
"这间屋子弥漫着一种淡淡的绿光，其来源我无法确定，使一切都清晰可见，尽管没有什么轮廓分明。我说一切，但实际上那光秃秃的石墙内唯一的物体是人类尸体。数目或许有八到十具——可以理解，我并未真正去数。他们年龄各异，或者说体型各异，从婴儿到成人，男女都有。除了一人之外，全都倒在地上。那是一个显然很年轻的女子，她坐着，背部倚靠在墙角。另一名更年长的妇女怀中紧抱着一个婴儿。一个半大男孩俯面趴在一名蓄着浓密胡须的男人的腿上。一两个人几乎赤身裸体，一个年轻女孩的手握着她已从胸前扯开的裙衫的碎片。尸体处于不同的腐烂阶段，面部和身形全都大幅萎缩。有些几乎只剩骨架。

===Original===
"While I stood stupefied with horror by this ghastly spectacle and still holding open the door, by some unaccountable perversity my attention was diverted from the shocking scene and concerned itself with trifles and details. Perhaps my mind, with an instinct of self-preservation, sought relief in matters which would relax its dangerous tension. Among other things, I observed that the door that I was holding open was of heavy iron plates, riveted. Equidistant from one another and from the top and bottom, three strong bolts protruded from the beveled edge. I turned the knob and they were retracted flush with the edge; released it, and they shot out. It was a spring lock. On the inside there was no knob, nor any kind of projection﻿—a smooth surface of iron.
"While noting these things with an interest and attention which it now astonishes me to recall I felt myself thrust aside, and Judge Veigh, whom in the intensity and vicissitudes of my feelings I had altogether forgotten, pushed by me into the room. 'For God's sake,' I cried, 'do not go in there! Let us get out of this dreadful place!'
"He gave no heed to my entreaties, but (as fearless a gentleman as lived in all the South) walked quickly to the center of the room, knelt beside one of the bodies for a closer examination and tenderly raised its blackened and shriveled head in his hands. A strong disagreeable odor came through the doorway, completely overpowering me. My senses reeled; I felt myself falling, and in clutching at the edge of the door for support pushed it shut with a sharp click!

===Chinese===
"当我因这恐怖景象而惊骇得呆若木鸡、仍握着门时，一种难以解释的乖僻使我的注意力从这骇人的场景转向了琐碎的细节。或许我的头脑出于自我保护的本能，在那些能松弛其危险紧张感的事物中寻求解脱。我注意到，我正握着的那扇门由铆接的厚铁板制成。三条坚固的门栓从斜边上等距突出，距顶部和底部也等距。我转了转门把手，它们便缩回与边沿齐平；一松手，它们便弹射出来。这是一个弹簧锁。里面没有把手，也没有任何突起——一片光滑的铁面。
"当我带着如今回想起来仍令我自己吃惊的兴趣和专注观察这些细节时，我感到自己被推到了一边，维格法官——我在情感的剧烈波动中已完全忘记了他——从我身旁挤进了房间。'看在上帝的份上，'我喊道，'别进去！让我们离开这个可怕的地方！'
"他没有理会我的恳求，而是（作为南方最无畏的绅士之一）快步走到房间中央，跪在一具尸体旁仔细查看，并温柔地将其焦黑、皱缩的头捧在手中。一股浓烈而难闻的气味从门口涌来，令我完全无法承受。我头晕目眩；我感到自己正在倒下，在抓住门边支撑自己时，砰的一声把门撞上了！

===Original===
"I remember no more: six weeks later I recovered my reason in a hotel at Manchester, whither I had been taken by strangers the next day. For all these weeks I had suffered from a nervous fever, attended with constant delirium. I had been found lying in the road several miles away from the house; but how I had escaped from it to get there I never knew. On recovery, or as soon as my physicians permitted me to talk, I inquired the fate of Judge Veigh, whom (to quiet me, as I now know) they represented as well and at home.
"No one believed a word of my story, and who can wonder? And who can imagine my grief when, arriving at my home in Frankfort two months later, I learned that Judge Veigh had never been heard of since that night? I then regretted bitterly the pride which since the first few days after the recovery of my reason had forbidden me to repeat my discredited story and insist upon its truth.
"With all that afterward occurred﻿—the examination of the house; the failure to find any room corresponding to that which I have described; the attempt to have me adjudged insane, and my triumph over my accusers﻿—the readers of the *Advocate* are familiar. After all these years I am still confident that excavations which I have neither the legal right to undertake nor the wealth to make would disclose the secret of the disappearance of my unhappy friend, and possibly of the former occupants and owners of the deserted and now destroyed house. I do not despair of yet bringing about such a search, and it is a source of deep grief to me that it has been delayed by the undeserved hostility and unwise incredulity of the family and friends of the late Judge Veigh."

===Chinese===
"此后我什么也不记得了：六周后我在曼彻斯特一家旅馆恢复了理智，我是前一天被陌生人带到那里的。这几个星期里我一直患有神经性热病，持续谵妄。人们在离那宅子几英里外的路上发现了我；但我是如何从那里逃出来的，我永远不得而知。康复后，或者说一旦医生允许我说话，我便询问维格法官的下落，而他们（如今我知道那是为了安抚我）说他安然无恙，已回到家中。
"没有人相信我说的哪怕一个字，这又能怪谁呢？谁又能想象我两个月后回到法兰克福家中、得知维格法官自那夜起便杳无音讯时的悲痛？我那时痛悔自己自恢复理智最初几天起便滋生的骄傲，它使我拒绝重述那已遭嗤笑的故事，坚持其真实性。
"此后发生的一切——对宅子的搜查；未能找到任何与我所描述的房间相符之处；试图将我判定为疯癫，以及我对指控者的胜利——《倡导者报》的读者们早已熟知。多年之后，我仍坚信，那些我既无合法权利也无财力进行的发掘，将会揭示我那位不幸朋友失踪的秘密，或许也能揭示这座如今已被焚毁的废弃宅邸此前的住户和主人失踪的秘密。我并未放弃促成此类搜寻的希望，而已故维格法官的家人和朋友因不应有的敌意和不明智的怀疑而使此事一再推迟，这令我深感痛心。"

===Original===
Colonel McArdle died in Frankfort on the thirteenth day of December, in the year 1879.

===Chinese===
麦卡德尔上校于1879年12月13日在法兰克福去世。

## The Other Lodgers / 其他房客

===Original===
"In order to take that train," said Colonel Levering, sitting in the Waldorf-Astoria hotel, "you will have to remain nearly all night in Atlanta. That is a fine city, but I advise you not to put up at the Breathitt House, one of the principal hotels. It is an old wooden building in urgent need of repairs. There are breaches in the walls that you could throw a cat through. The bedrooms have no locks on the doors, no furniture but a single chair in each, and a bedstead without bedding—just a mattress. Even these meager accommodations you cannot be sure that you will have in monopoly; you must take your chance of being stowed in with a lot of others. Sir, it is a most abominable hotel.

"The night that I passed in it was an uncomfortable night. I got in late and was shown to my room on the ground floor by an apologetic night-clerk with a tallow candle, which he considerately left with me. I was worn out by two days and a night of hard railway travel and had not entirely recovered from a gunshot wound in the head, received in an altercation. Rather than look for better quarters I lay down on the mattress without removing my clothing and fell asleep.

"Along toward morning I awoke. The moon had risen and was shining in at the uncurtained window, illuminating the room with a soft, bluish light which seemed, somehow, a bit spooky, though I dare say it had no uncommon quality; all moonlight is that way if you will observe it. Imagine my surprise and indignation when I saw the floor occupied by at least a dozen other lodgers! I sat up, earnestly damning the management of that unthinkable hotel, and was about to spring from the bed to go and make trouble for the night-clerk—him of the apologetic manner and the tallow candle—when something in the situation affected me with a strange indisposition to move. I suppose I was what a story-writer might call 'frozen with terror.' For those men were obviously all dead!

===Chinese===
"要赶那趟火车的话，"莱弗林上校（Colonel Levering）坐在华尔道夫-阿斯托里亚酒店里说道，"你得在亚特兰大待上大半夜。那是个不错的城市，但我劝你别住布雷西特旅馆（Breathitt House），那是当地最主要的酒店之一。一幢急需修缮的老木楼，墙上的裂缝能塞进一只猫。卧室的门没有锁，除了每间一把椅子外没有任何家具，床架上没有被褥——只有一张床垫。就连这寒酸的配备你也未必能独享；你得碰运气，看会不会跟一堆人挤在一起。先生，那是一家令人发指的旅馆。

"我在那儿度过的那个晚上可不舒服。我到得很晚，一个满脸歉意的夜班接待员举着一支牛油蜡烛把我领到一楼的房间，还好心地把蜡烛留给了我。连续两天一夜的火车颠簸让我筋疲力尽，而且头上的枪伤——在一次争吵中挨的——还没完全好。我懒得再去找更好的住处，和衣躺在床垫上就睡着了。

"快天亮的时候我醒了。月亮已经升起来了，透过没挂窗帘的窗户照进来，给房间蒙上一层柔和的、泛蓝的月光，不知怎的显得有点阴森——不过我敢说这光并无特别之处；你若仔细观察，所有月光都是那副模样。请想象我看到地板上至少躺了十几个其他房客时的震惊和愤怒！我坐起来，痛骂这家不可思议的旅馆的管理层，正要从床上跳起来去找那个夜班接待员算账——那个满脸歉意、拿着牛油蜡烛的家伙——这时某种情形让我突然莫名其妙地不想动弹了。我想这就是小说家所说的'吓得僵住了'。因为那些人显然全都已经死了！

===Original===
"They lay on their backs, disposed orderly along three sides of the room, their feet to the walls—against the other wall, farthest from the door, stood my bed and the chair. All the faces were covered, but under their white cloths the features of the two bodies that lay in the square patch of moonlight near the window showed in sharp profile as to nose and chin.

"I thought this a bad dream and tried to cry out, as one does in a nightmare, but could make no sound. At last, with a desperate effort I threw my feet to the floor and passing between the two rows of clouted faces and the two bodies that lay nearest the door, I escaped from the infernal place and ran to the office. The night-clerk was there, behind the desk, sitting in the dim light of another tallow candle—just sitting and staring. He did not rise: my abrupt entrance produced no effect upon him, though I must have looked a veritable corpse myself. It occurred to me then that I had not before really observed the fellow. He was a little chap, with a colorless face and the whitest, blanched eyes I ever saw. He had no more expression than the back of my hand. His clothing was a dirty gray.

"'Damn you!' I said; 'what do you mean?'

"Just the same, I was shaking like a leaf in the wind and did not recognize my own voice.

"The night-clerk rose, bowed (apologetically) and—well, he was no longer there, and at that moment I felt a hand laid upon my shoulder from behind. Just fancy that if you can! Unspeakably frightened, I turned and saw a portly, kind-faced gentleman, who asked:

"'What is the matter, my friend?'

===Chinese===
"他们仰面朝天，整整齐齐地排列在房间三面，脚朝着墙壁——我的床和那把椅子靠在离门最远的那面墙边。所有的脸都被盖住了，但白布下面，躺在窗户旁那块方形月光中的两具尸体，鼻子和下巴的轮廓清晰可辨。

"我以为这是一场噩梦，像在噩梦中那样试图喊叫，却发不出声音。最后，我拼尽全力把脚伸到地上，从两排蒙着布的面孔和那两具离门最近的尸体之间挤过去，逃离了那个地狱般的地方，跑到办公室。夜班接待员在那里，在柜台后面，坐在另一支牛油蜡烛的昏暗光线下——就那么坐着，两眼发直。他没有站起来：我突然闯入对他毫无影响，尽管我自己看起来也活脱脱像具尸体。这时我才想起以前从未真正注意过这家伙。他是个小个子，脸色苍白，眼睛是我见过的最白、最死白的颜色。他的表情和我的手背一样僵硬。衣服脏兮兮的，灰不溜秋。

"'你搞什么鬼！'我说，'你到底什么意思？'

"尽管如此，我还是像风中的树叶一样浑身发抖，连自己的声音都听不出来了。

"夜班接待员站起来，鞠了一躬（带着歉意），然后——他就不见了，就在这时，我感到一只手从后面搭上了我的肩膀。请你想象一下那是什么感觉！我吓得魂飞魄散，转过身来，看到一位和蔼可亲的胖绅士，他问道：

"'怎么了，我的朋友？'

===Original===
"I was not long in telling him, but before I made an end of it he went pale himself. 'See here,' he said, 'are you telling the truth?'

"I had now got myself in hand and terror had given place to indignation. 'If you dare to doubt it,' I said, 'I'll hammer the life out of you!'

"'No,' he replied, 'don't do that; just sit down till I tell you. This is not a hotel. It used to be; afterward it was a hospital. Now it is unoccupied, awaiting a tenant. The room that you mention was the dead-room—there were always plenty of dead. The fellow that you call the night-clerk used to be that, but later he booked the patients as they were brought in. I don't understand his being here. He has been dead a few weeks.'

"'And who are you?' I blurted out.

"'Oh, I look after the premises. I happened to be passing just now, and seeing a light in here came in to investigate. Let us have a look into that room,' he added, lifting the sputtering candle from the desk.

"'I'll see you at the devil first!' said I, bolting out of the door into the street.

"Sir, that Breathitt House, in Atlanta, is a beastly place! Don't you stop there."

===Chinese===
"我很快就跟他说完了，但还没等我说完，他自己也脸色发白了。'我说，'他说，'你说的是真的吗？'

"我此时已经控制住了自己，恐惧让位给了愤怒。'你敢怀疑的话，'我说，'我就把你揍扁！'

"'不，'他回答，'别那样；坐下来听我说。这里不是旅馆。它曾经是；后来成了医院。现在空着，等着租出去。你说的那个房间是停尸房——总有死人。你说的那个夜班接待员以前确实是干这个的，但后来也负责登记送进来的病人。我搞不懂他怎么会在这里。他死了有几个星期了。'

"'那你又是谁？'我脱口问道。

"'噢，我负责照管这地方。我刚好路过，看到这里有灯就进来看看。咱们去那个房间瞧瞧，'他补了一句，从桌上拿起那支噼啪作响的蜡烛。

"'你先下地狱去吧！'我说完就冲出门跑到街上去了。

"先生，亚特兰大的那个布雷西特旅馆是个可怕的鬼地方！你千万别在那儿落脚。"

===Original===
"God forbid! Your account of it certainly does not suggest comfort. By the way, Colonel, when did all that occur?"

"In September, 1864—shortly after the siege."

===Chinese===
"上帝保佑！你这番描述确实让人觉得不怎么舒适。对了，上校，这一切是什么时候发生的？"

"1864年9月——就在围城之后不久。"

## The Thing at Nolan / 诺兰镇的那个东西

===Original===
To the south of where the road between Leesville and Hardy, in the State of Missouri, crosses the east fork of May Creek stands an abandoned house. Nobody has lived in it since the summer of 1879, and it is fast going to pieces. For some three years before the date mentioned above, it was occupied by the family of Charles May, from one of whose ancestors the creek near which it stands took its name.

===Chinese===
在密苏里州（Missouri），利斯维尔（Leesville）与哈迪（Hardy）之间的道路穿过梅溪（May Creek）东叉的地方，坐落着一座废弃的房屋。自一八七九年的夏天起，便无人在此居住，它正迅速坍塌。在上述日期之前大约三年，这里住着查尔斯·梅（Charles May）一家，他的一位祖先的名字成了这条小溪的名称。

===Original===
Mr. May's family consisted of a wife, an adult son and two young girls. The son's name was John—the names of the daughters are unknown to the writer of this sketch.

===Chinese===
梅先生一家包括妻子、一个成年的儿子和两个年幼的女儿。儿子叫约翰（John）——女儿们的名字，本文作者不得而知。

===Original===
John May was of a morose and surly disposition, not easily moved to anger, but having an uncommon gift of sullen, implacable hate. His father was quite otherwise; of a sunny, jovial disposition, but with a quick temper like a sudden flame kindled in a wisp of straw, which consumes it in a flash and is no more. He cherished no resentments, and his anger gone, was quick to make overtures for reconciliation. He had a brother living near by who was unlike him in respect of all this, and it was a current witticism in the neighborhood that John had inherited his disposition from his uncle.

===Chinese===
约翰·梅性情阴郁乖戾，不易动怒，却有着一种罕见的、冷酷而不可化解的怨恨天赋。他的父亲则截然相反；性情开朗快活，但脾气暴烈，如同点燃在一把干草中的烈焰，瞬间将其吞噬殆尽，随即消失。他不记仇，怒气一消便迅速主动求和。他有一个住在附近的兄弟，在所有这些方面都与他不同，邻里间流传的一个玩笑是，约翰的性子是从他叔叔那里遗传来的。

===Original===
One day a misunderstanding arose between father and son, harsh words ensued, and the father struck the son full in the face with his fist. John quietly wiped away the blood that followed the blow, fixed his eyes upon the already penitent offender and said with cold composure, "You will die for that."

===Chinese===
一天，父子之间发生了误会，言语激烈起来，父亲一拳打在儿子脸上。约翰平静地擦去随之流出的鲜血，冷冷地盯着已经后悔的父亲，沉着地说："你会为此付出代价。"

===Original===
The words were overheard by two brothers named Jackson, who were approaching the men at the moment; but seeing them engaged in a quarrel they retired, apparently unobserved. Charles May afterward related the unfortunate occurrence to his wife and explained that he had apologized to the son for the hasty blow, but without avail; the young man not only rejected his overtures, but refused to withdraw his terrible threat. Nevertheless, there was no open rupture of relations: John continued living with the family, and things went on very much as before.

===Chinese===
这番话被两个姓杰克逊（Jackson）的兄弟无意中听到，他们当时正朝这两人走来；但看到他们正在争吵，便退开了，似乎没有被察觉。查尔斯·梅后来把这起不幸的事件告诉了妻子，并解释说他已为那一时冲动的耳光向儿子道了歉，但无济于事；那个年轻人不仅拒绝了他的和解，而且不肯收回他那可怕的威胁。然而，公开的关系并未破裂：约翰继续与家人同住，一切照旧。

===Original===
One Sunday morning in June, 1879, about two weeks after what has been related, May senior left the house immediately after breakfast, taking a spade. He said he was going to make an excavation at a certain spring in a wood about a mile away, so that the cattle could obtain water. John remained in the house for some hours, variously occupied in shaving himself, writing letters and reading a newspaper. His manner was very nearly what it usually was; perhaps he was a trifle more sullen and surly.

===Chinese===
一八七九年六月的一个星期天上午，大约在所述事件发生两周后，老梅早餐后立刻带着一把铁锹离开了家。他说要去约一英里外树林中的某个泉眼挖个坑，好让牲口能喝到水。约翰在屋里待了几个小时，忙着刮胡子、写信和看报纸。他的举止几乎和往常一样；也许只是更阴郁了一些。

===Original===
At two o'clock he left the house. At five, he returned. For some reason not connected with any interest in his movements, and which is not now recalled, the time of his departure and that of his return were noted by his mother and sisters, as was attested at his trial for murder. It was observed that his clothing was wet in spots, as if (so the prosecution afterward pointed out) he had been removing bloodstains from it. His manner was strange, his look wild. He complained of illness, and going to his room took to his bed.

===Chinese===
下午两点他离开了家。五点他回来了。出于某些与他的行踪无关且现在已无法回忆的原因，他离开和返回的时间被母亲和姐妹们记下了，这一点在他后来受谋杀罪审判时得到了证实。人们注意到他的衣服上有几处湿痕，正如控方后来指出的，那像是他曾试图洗去上面的血迹。他的举止异常，神情恍惚。他抱怨身体不适，走进自己的房间躺下了。

===Original===
May senior did not return. Later that evening the nearest neighbors were aroused, and during that night and the following day a search was prosecuted through the wood where the spring was. It resulted in little but the discovery of both men's footprints in the clay about the spring. John May in the meantime had grown rapidly worse with what the local physician called brain fever, and in his delirium raved of murder, but did not say whom he conceived to have been murdered, nor whom he imagined to have done the deed. But his threat was recalled by the brothers Jackson and he was arrested on suspicion and a deputy sheriff put in charge of him at his home. Public opinion ran strongly against him and but for his illness he would probably have been hanged by a mob. As it was, a meeting of the neighbors was held on Tuesday and a committee appointed to watch the case and take such action at any time as circumstances might seem to warrant.

===Chinese===
老梅没有回来。那天晚上稍晚时候，最近的邻居们被惊动了，当晚和第二天一整天，搜寻队在那个泉眼所在的树林中搜索。除了在泉眼周围的泥土中发现两人的脚印外，几乎一无所获。与此同时，约翰·梅的病情迅速恶化，当地医生称之为脑热病，他在谵妄中胡言乱语着谋杀的事，但没有说出他认为谁被杀了，也没有说出他认为谁是凶手。但杰克逊兄弟想起了他的威胁，他因涉嫌犯罪被捕，一名副治安官被派到他家中看管他。舆论对他极为不利，若非他身患重病，他很可能已被暴民处以私刑。无论如何，邻居们在星期二举行了一次会议，任命了一个委员会，负责监视此案并在任何情况下采取适当的行动。

===Original===
On Wednesday all was changed. From the town of Nolan, eight miles away, came a story which put a quite different light on the matter. Nolan consisted of a school house, a blacksmith's shop, a "store" and a half-dozen dwellings. The store was kept by one Henry Odell, a cousin of the elder May. On the afternoon of the Sunday of May's disappearance Mr. Odell and four of his neighbors, men of credibility, were sitting in the store smoking and talking. It was a warm day; and both the front and the back door were open. At about three o'clock Charles May, who was well known to three of them, entered at the front door and passed out at the rear. He was without hat or coat. He did not look at them, nor return their greeting, a circumstance which did not surprise, for he was evidently seriously hurt. Above the left eyebrow was a wound—a deep gash from which the blood flowed, covering the whole left side of the face and neck and saturating his light-gray shirt. Oddly enough, the thought uppermost in the minds of all was that he had been fighting and was going to the brook directly at the back of the store, to wash himself.

===Chinese===
星期三，一切发生了转变。来自八英里外诺兰镇（Nolan）的消息给这件事带来了截然不同的解释。诺兰镇有一所校舍、一家铁匠铺、一间"店铺"和半打住宅。店铺由亨利·奥德尔（Henry Odell）经营，他是老梅的表亲。梅失踪的那个星期天下午，奥德尔先生和四位信誉良好的邻居坐在店里抽烟聊天。天气很热，前后门都敞开着。大约三点钟，查尔斯·梅——他们中有三人认识他——从前门进来，从后门出去。他没戴帽子也没穿外套。他没有看他们，也没有回礼，这并不令人意外，因为他显然受了重伤。他的左眉上方有一道伤口——一道深深的裂口，鲜血直流，覆盖了整个左侧脸庞和脖颈，浸透了他那件浅灰色的衬衫。奇怪的是，所有人脑海中浮现的第一个念头是他刚才打了架，要去店铺后面的小溪洗一洗。

===Original===
Perhaps there was a feeling of delicacy—a backwoods etiquette which restrained them from following him to offer assistance; the court records, from which, mainly, this narrative is drawn, are silent as to anything but the fact. They waited for him to return, but he did not return.

===Chinese===
或许出于某种微妙的感觉——一种边远地区的礼节——他们没有跟着他去提供帮助；本文主要依据的法庭记录只记载了这一事实，对其他事情保持沉默。他们等他回来，但他没有回来。

===Original===
Bordering the brook behind the store is a forest extending for six miles back to the Medicine Lodge Hills. As soon as it became known in the neighborhood of the missing man's dwelling that he had been seen in Nolan there was a marked alteration in public sentiment and feeling. The vigilance committee went out of existence without the formality of a resolution. Search along the wooded bottom lands of May Creek was stopped and nearly the entire male population of the region took to beating the bush about Nolan and in the Medicine Lodge Hills. But of the missing man no trace was found.

===Chinese===
店铺后面的小溪旁是一片森林，向后延伸六英里直到梅迪辛洛奇山（Medicine Lodge Hills）。失踪者住所附近的人们一听说他在诺兰镇被看到，公众舆论和情绪就发生了显著变化。治安维持会在没有任何正式决议的情况下便解散了。在梅溪沿岸的林间低地进行的搜索停止了，该地区几乎所有的男性都去诺兰镇周围和梅迪辛洛奇山中搜寻。但失踪者没有留下任何踪迹。

===Original===
One of the strangest circumstances of this strange case is the formal indictment and trial of a man for murder of one whose body no human being professed to have seen—one not known to be dead. We are all more or less familiar with the vagaries and eccentricities of frontier law, but this instance, it is thought, is unique. However that may be, it is of record that on recovering from his illness John May was indicted for the murder of his missing father. Counsel for the defense appears not to have demurred and the case was tried on its merits. The prosecution was spiritless and perfunctory; the defense easily established—with regard to the deceased—an alibi. If during the time in which John May must have killed Charles May, if he killed him at all, Charles May was miles away from where John May must have been, it is plain that the deceased must have come to his death at the hands of someone else.

===Chinese===
这桩奇案中最奇异的情形之一，是对一个人提起正式起诉并审判其谋杀了一个连尸体都无人声称见过、连是否已死都不确定的人。我们多少都熟悉边疆法律的反复无常和荒诞离奇，但人们认为这个案例是绝无仅有的。无论怎样，有记录表明，约翰·梅病愈后被起诉谋杀了他失踪的父亲。辩护律师似乎没有提出异议，案件被交付实质审理。控方显得无精打采、敷衍了事；辩方轻而易举地——就死者而言——建立了不在场证明。如果在约翰·梅本应杀害查尔斯·梅的那段时间内，如果他确实杀了人的话，查尔斯·梅远在约翰·梅所在之处数英里之外，那么很明显，死者必定是死于他人之手。

===Original===
John May was acquitted, immediately left the country, and has never been heard of from that day. Shortly afterward his mother and sisters removed to St. Louis. The farm having passed into the possession of a man who owns the land adjoining, and has a dwelling of his own, the May house has ever since been vacant, and has the somber reputation of being haunted.

===Chinese===
约翰·梅被判无罪，立即离开了这个国家，从此杳无音信。不久之后，他的母亲和姐妹们搬到了圣路易斯（St. Louis）。农场转入了一个拥有相邻土地并自有住宅的人手中，梅家的房子自此一直空着，有着闹鬼的阴森名声。

===Original===
One day after the May family had left the country, some boys, playing in the woods along May Creek, found concealed under a mass of dead leaves, but partly exposed by the rooting of hogs, a spade, nearly new and bright, except for a spot on one edge, which was rusted and stained with blood. The implement had the initials C. M. cut into the handle.

===Chinese===
梅一家离开后某日，几个男孩在梅溪沿岸的树林里玩耍，在一堆枯叶下发现了一把铁锹，被猪拱土时翻出了一部分。铁锹几乎全新，光亮如初，只有一处边缘有锈迹和血污。锹柄上刻着缩写字母C. M.。

===Original===
This discovery renewed, in some degree, the public excitement of a few months before. The earth near the spot where the spade was found was carefully examined, and the result was the finding of the dead body of a man. It had been buried under two or three feet of soil and the spot covered with a layer of dead leaves and twigs. There was but little decomposition, a fact attributed to some preservative property in the mineral-bearing soil.

===Chinese===
这一发现在一定程度上重新激起了几个月前的公众兴奋。铁锹发现地附近的泥土被仔细检查，结果发现了一具男尸。尸体被埋在两三英尺深的土壤下，上面覆盖着一层枯叶和树枝。尸体几乎未腐烂，这一现象被归因于含矿物质的土壤具有某种防腐特性。

===Original===
Above the left eyebrow was a wound—a deep gash from which blood had flowed, covering the whole left side of the face and neck and saturating the light-gray shirt. The skull had been cut through by the blow. The body was that of Charles May.

===Chinese===
左眉上方有一道伤口——一道深深的裂口，鲜血曾从此流出，覆盖了整个左侧脸庞和脖颈，浸透了浅灰色的衬衫。头骨被这一击劈开。这具尸体正是查尔斯·梅。

===Original===
But what was it that passed through Mr. Odell's store at Nolan?

===Chinese===
但是，那个穿过诺兰镇奥德尔先生店铺的，又是什么呢？

===Original===
A. B.

===Chinese===
A. B.

### That of Granny Magone / 格拉尼·马贡的故事

===Original===
About ten miles to the southeast of Whitesburg, Ky., in a little “cove” of the Cumberland mountains, lived for many years an old woman named Sarah (or Mary) Magone. Her house, built of logs and containing but two rooms, was a mile and a half distant from any other, in the wildest part of the “cove,” entirely surrounded by forest except on one side, where a little field, or “patch,” of about a half-acre served her for a vegetable garden. How she subsisted nobody exactly knew; she was reputed to be a miser with a concealed hoard; she certainly paid for what few articles she procured on her rare visits to the village store. Many of her ignorant neighbors believed her to be a witch, or thought, at least, that she possessed some kind of supernatural powers. In November, 1881, she died, and fortunately enough, the body was found while yet warm by a passing hunter, who locked the door of the cabin and conveyed the news to the nearest settlement.

===Chinese===
在肯塔基州怀茨堡（Whitesburg）东南约十英里处，坎伯兰山脉（Cumberland mountains）的一个小“谷地”里，住着一位名叫萨拉（或玛丽）·马贡（Sarah (or Mary) Magone）的老妇人，已居住多年。她的房子是原木搭建的，只有两个房间，离其他任何人家都有一英里半远，位于“谷地”最荒凉的地方，除了房子一侧有一小块约半英亩的田地，或称“菜圃”，用作她的菜园，四周完全被森林环绕。没人确切知道她是如何维生的；据信她是个守财奴，藏有积蓄；她确实为偶尔去村子里的商店买来的少数几样东西付了钱。她那些无知的邻居中许多人相信她是个女巫，或者至少认为她拥有某种超自然的力量。1881年11月，她死了，幸运的是，一个路过的猎人发现了尚有余温的尸体，他锁上小木屋的门，将消息带到了最近的聚居地。

===Original===
Several persons living in the vicinity at once went to the cabin to prepare for her burial; others were to follow the next day with a coffin and whatever else was needful. Among those who first went was the Rev. Elias Atney, a Methodist minister of Whitesburg, who happened to be in the neighborhood visiting a relation. He was to conduct the funeral services on the following day. Mr. Atney is, or was, well known in Whitesburg and all that country as a good and pious man of good birth and education. He was closely related to the Marshalls and several other families of distinction. It is from him that the particulars here related were learned; and the account is confirmed by the affidavits of John Hershaw, William C. Wrightman, and Catharine Doub, residents of the vicinity and eyewitnesses.

===Chinese===
附近住着的几个人立刻前往小木屋，为她的葬礼做准备；其他人第二天会带着棺材和其他必需品前来。最先去的人中有怀茨堡卫理公会牧师伊莱亚斯·阿特尼牧师（Rev. Elias Atney），他碰巧在附近拜访一位亲戚。他将在第二天主持葬礼。阿特尼先生在怀茨堡及整个地区都很知名，是一个出身良好、受过教育、善良虔诚的人。他与马歇尔家族（Marshalls）以及另外几个显赫家族关系密切。上述细节正是从他那里得知的；所述情况得到了附近居民兼目击者约翰·赫肖（John Hershaw）、威廉·C·赖特曼（William C. Wrightman）和凯瑟琳·道布（Catharine Doub）的宣誓书的证实。

===Original===
The body of “Granny” Magone had been “laid out” on a wide plank supported by two chairs at the end of the principal room, opposite the fireplace, and the persons mentioned were acting as “watchers,” according to the local custom. A bright fire on the hearth lighted one end of the room brilliantly, the other dimly. The watchers sat about the fire, talking in subdued tones, when a sudden noise in the direction of the corpse caused them all to turn and look. In a black shadow near the remains, they saw two glowing eyes staring fixedly; and before they could do more than rise, uttering exclamations of alarm, a large black cat leaped upon the body and fastened its teeth into the cloth covering the face. Instantly the right hand of the dead was violently raised from the side, seized the cat, and hurled it against the wall, whence it fell to the floor, and then dashed wildly through an open window into the outer darkness, and was seen no more.

===Chinese===
“格拉尼”·马贡的尸体已被“停放”在一张宽木板上，木板由两把椅子支撑着，放在主房间尽头，正对着壁炉，上述人员按照当地习俗担任“守灵人”。壁炉里明亮的火光照亮了房间的一端，另一端则光线昏暗。守灵人围坐在火炉旁，压低声音交谈着，这时尸体方向突然传来一声响动，他们都转身看去。在尸体附近的一个黑影中，他们看到两只发光的眼睛死死盯着；还没等他们来得及站起来发出惊叫，一只大黑猫就跳到了尸体上，牙齿咬住了盖在脸上的布。瞬间，死者的右手猛地从身侧抬起，抓住猫，将它摔向墙壁，猫从墙上跌落到地板上，然后疯狂地冲出一扇开着的窗户，消失在户外的黑暗中，再也不见踪影。

===Original===
Inconceivably horrified, the watchers stood a moment speechless; but finally, with returning courage, approached the body. The face-cloth lay upon the floor; the cheek was terribly torn; the right arm hung stiffly over the side of the plank. There was not a sign of life. They chafed the forehead, the withered cheeks and neck. They carried the body to the heat of the fire and worked upon it for hours: all in vain. But the funeral was postponed until the fourth day brought unmistakable evidence of dissolution, and poor Granny was buried.

===Chinese===
守灵人惊恐得难以言表，一时呆立无言；但最终，鼓起勇气，走近了尸体。面巾掉在地板上；脸颊被严重抓伤；右臂僵硬地垂在木板边。没有任何生命迹象。他们揉搓着额头、干瘪的脸颊和脖子。他们把尸体抬到火炉旁，在上面忙活了好几个小时：全都徒劳。葬礼推迟到第四天，那时出现了明显腐烂的迹象，可怜的格拉尼被埋葬了。

===Original===
“Ah, but your eyes deceived you,” said he to whom the reverend gentleman related the occurrence. “The arm was disturbed by the efforts of the cat, which, taking sudden fright, leaped blindly against the wall.”

===Chinese===
“啊，但你的眼睛欺骗了你，”那位绅士牧师向他叙述这件事时，听者说道。“那只手臂是被猫的挣扎弄动的，猫突然受惊，盲目地跳向墙壁。”

===Original===
“No,” he answered, “the clenched right hand, with its long nails, was full of black fur.”

===Chinese===
“不，”他回答道，“那只紧握的右手，留着长长的指甲，里面全是黑色的毛。”

## A Light Sleeper / 轻睡的人

===Original===
John Hoskin, living in San Francisco, had a beautiful wife, to whom he was devotedly attached. In the spring of 1871 Mrs. Hoskin went East to visit her relations in Springfield, Ill., where, a week after her arrival, she suddenly died of some disease of the heart; at least the physician said so. Mr. Hoskin was at once apprised of his loss, by telegraph, and he directed that the body be sent to San Francisco. On arrival there the metallic case containing the remains was opened. The body was lying on the right side, the right hand under the cheek, the other on the breast. The posture was the perfectly natural one of a sleeping child, and in a letter to the deceased lady's father, Mr. Martin L. Whitney of Spring field, Mr. Hoskin expressed a grateful sense of the thoughtfulness that had so composed the remains as to soften the suggestion of death. To his surprise he learned from the father that nothing of the kind had been done: the body had been put in the casket in the customary way, lying on the back, with the arms extended along the sides. In the meantime the casket had been deposited in the receiving vault at Laurel Hill Cemetery, awaiting the completion of a tomb.

===Chinese===
住在旧金山的约翰·霍斯金（John Hoskin）有一位美丽的妻子，对她一往情深。1871年春天，霍斯金太太前往东部探望她在伊利诺伊州斯普林菲尔德（Springfield, Ill.）的亲戚，抵达一周后，她因某种心脏病突然去世了——至少医生是这么说的。霍斯金先生立刻接到电报得知了噩耗，他指示将遗体送往旧金山。遗体运抵后，装着遗骸的金属棺被打开了。尸体侧卧在右边，右手垫在脸颊下，另一只手放在胸前。这姿势就像熟睡的孩子一样自然。霍斯金先生在写给已故女士的父亲——斯普林菲尔德的马丁·L·惠特尼（Martin L. Whitney）先生——的信中，表达了对对方如此精心安放遗体以减轻死亡之感的深切谢意。令他惊讶的是，他从那位父亲那里得知根本没有人这样做过：遗体是按通常方式放入棺木的，仰面朝天，双臂伸展在身体两侧。在此期间，棺木已被安置在月桂山公墓（Laurel Hill Cemetery）的接收室中，等待陵墓的修建完工。

===Original===
Greatly disquieted by this revelation, Hoskin did not at once reflect that the easy and natural posture and placid expression precluded the idea of suspended animation, subsequent revival, and eventual death by suffocation. He insisted that his wife had been murdered by medical incompetency and heedless haste. Under the influence of this feeling he wrote to Mr. Whitney again, expressing in passionate terms his horror and renewed grief. Some days afterward, someone having suggested that the casket had been opened en route, probably in the hope of plunder, and pointing out the impossibility of the change having occurred in the straitened space of the con fining metal, it was resolved to reopen it.

===Chinese===
霍斯金被这一发现搅得心神不宁，一时没有去想那轻松自然的姿势和平静的表情恰恰排除了假死、随后复苏、最终窒息而死的可能性。他坚持认为妻子是被无能的医疗和草率的疏忽害死的。在这种情绪的驱使下，他又给惠特尼先生写了一封信，以激烈的言辞表达了他的恐惧和重燃的悲痛。几天后，有人提出棺木可能在途中被打开过，大概是图谋劫掠，并指出在封闭的金属棺那狭小的空间里，尸体不可能自行变换位置，于是决定重新打开棺木。

===Original===
Removal of the lid disclosed a new horror: the body now lay upon its *left* side. The position was cramped, and to a living person would have been uncomfortable. The face wore an expression of pain. Some costly rings on the fingers were undisturbed. Overcome by his emotions, to which was now added a sharp, if mistaken, remorse, Mr. Hoskin lost his reason, dying years afterward in the asylum at Stockton.

===Chinese===
揭开棺盖，映入眼帘的是新的恐怖：尸体现在侧卧在*左*边。姿势很局促，对活人来说会很不舒服。脸上带着痛苦的表情。手指上的几枚贵重戒指纹丝未动。霍斯金先生被自己的情绪压垮了——其中又加上了尖锐却错误的悔恨——他精神崩溃了，数年后死于斯托克顿（Stockton）的疯人院。

===Original===
A physician having been summoned, to assist in clearing up the mystery, viewed the body of the dead woman, pronounced life obviously extinct, and ordered the casket closed for the third and last time. "Obviously extinct," indeed: the corpse had, in fact, been embalmed at Springfield.

===Chinese===
应召前来协助揭开谜团的一位医生查看了死去女人的尸体，宣布生命迹象显然已完全消失，并下令将棺木第三次也是最后一次合上。"显然已完全消失"——的确如此：事实上，那具尸体在斯普林菲尔德就已经被防腐处理过了。

### The Mystery of Charles Farquharson / 查尔斯·法夸尔森之谜

===Original===
One night in the summer of 1843 William Hayner Gordon, of Philadelphia, lay in his bed reading Goldsmith's "Traveler," by the light of a candle. It was about eleven o'clock. The room was in the third story of the house and had two windows looking out upon Chestnut Street; there was no balcony, nothing below the windows but other windows in a smooth brick wall.

Becoming drowsy, Gordon laid away his book, extinguished his candle, and composed himself to sleep. A moment later (as he afterward averred) he remembered that he had neglected to place his watch within reach, and rose in the dark to get it from the pocket of his waistcoat, which he had hung on the back of a chair on the opposite side of the room, near one of the windows. In crossing, his foot came in contact with some heavy object and he was thrown to the floor. Rising, he struck a match and lighted his candle. In the center of the room lay the corpse of a man.

Gordon was no coward, as he afterward proved by his gallant death upon the enemy's parapet at Chapultepec, but this strange apparition of a human corpse where but a moment before, as he believed, there had been nothing, was too much for his nerves, and he cried aloud. Henri Granier, who occupied an adjoining room, but had not retired, came instantly to Gordon's door and attempted to enter. The door being bolted, and Gordon too greatly agitated to open it, Granier burst it in.

Gordon was taken into custody and an inquest held, but what has been related was all that could be ascertained. The most diligent efforts on the part of the police and the press failed to identify the dead. Physicians testifying at the inquest agreed that death had occurred but a few hours before the discovery, but none was able to divine the cause; all the organs of the body were in an apparently healthy condition; there were no traces of either violence or poison.

===Chinese===
1843年夏天的一个晚上，费城的威廉·海纳·戈登（William Hayner Gordon）躺在床上，借着烛光阅读戈德史密斯的《旅行者》（Goldsmith's "Traveler"）。大约十一点钟。房间在房子的三楼，有两扇窗户朝向栗树街（Chestnut Street）；没有阳台，窗户下方除了光滑砖墙上的其他窗外什么也没有。

戈登感到困倦，便放下书，吹灭蜡烛，准备睡觉。过了一会儿（正如他后来断言的那样），他想起忘了把手表放在够得着的地方，于是在黑暗中起身，从他挂在房间对面靠近一扇窗户的椅子背上的马甲口袋里取手表。在穿过房间时，他的脚碰到了某个沉重的东西，被绊倒在地。他爬起来，划了一根火柴，点燃了蜡烛。房间中央躺着一具男人的尸体。

戈登并非懦夫，这一点后来他在查普尔特佩克（Chapultepec）敌人胸墙上的英勇牺牲中得到了证明，但就在片刻之前，据他所信，这里空无一物，而现在却出现了这具人类尸体的奇怪幻影，这超出了他的神经承受能力，他大声呼救。亨利·格拉尼耶（Henri Granier）住在隔壁房间，但尚未就寝，他立刻来到戈登的门边，试图进入。门闩着，戈登又激动得无法开门，格拉尼耶便把门撞开了。

戈登被拘留并进行了验尸，但除了所述内容外，无法查明更多情况。警方和媒体最勤奋的努力也未能确认死者身份。在验尸中作证的医生们一致认为死亡发生在发现前仅几小时，但无人能查明原因；身体的所有器官看起来都很健康；没有暴力或毒药的痕迹。

===Original===
Eight or ten months later Gordon received a letter from Charles Ritcher in Bombay, relating the death in that city of Charles Farquharson, whom both Gordon and Ritcher had known when all were boys. Enclosed in the letter was a daguerreotype of the deceased, found among his effects. As nearly as the living can look like the dead it was an exact likeness of the mysterious body found in Gordon's bedroom, and it was with a strange feeling that Gordon observed that the death, making allowance for the difference of time, was said to have occurred on the very night of the adventure. He wrote for further particulars, with especial reference to what disposition had been made of Farquharson's body.

"You know he turned Parsee," wrote Ritcher in reply; "so his naked remains were exposed on the grating of the Tower of Silence, as those of all good Parsees are. I saw the buzzards fighting for them and gorging themselves helpless on his fragments."

On some pretense Gordon and his friends obtained authority to open the dead man's grave. The coffin had evidently not been disturbed. They unscrewed the lid. The shroud was a trifle moldy. There was no body nor any vestige of one.

===Chinese===
八到十个月后，戈登收到了查尔斯·里彻（Charles Ritcher）从孟买寄来的一封信，信中讲述了查尔斯·法夸尔森（Charles Farquharson）在该市去世的消息，戈登和里彻在年少时都认识他。信中附有一张死者的银版照片，在其遗物中发现。活人看上去与死者相像的程度有限，但这张照片与戈登卧室中发现的那具神秘尸体极为相似，戈登怀着一种奇怪的感觉注意到，考虑到时间差异，据说死亡发生在那个冒险之夜的同一天晚上。他写信询问更多细节，特别是关于法夸尔森尸体的处理情况。

"你知道他改信了帕西教（Parsee），"里彻回信写道；"所以他的裸露遗体被陈列在静默之塔（Tower of Silence）的格栅上，就像所有虔诚的帕西教徒那样。我看到秃鹫为了他的遗体而争斗，贪婪地吞食他的碎片，直到自己动弹不得。"

戈登和他的朋友们以某种借口获得了打开这座坟墓的许可。棺材显然未被打开过。他们拧开棺盖。裹尸布有点发霉。没有尸体，也没有任何痕迹。

### Dead and “Gone” / 死去并“消失”

===Original===
On the morning of the 14th day of August, 1872, George J. Reid, a young man of twenty-one years, living at Xenia, O., fell while walking across the dining room in his father’s house. The family consisted of his father, mother, two sisters, and a cousin, a boy of fifteen. All were present at the breakfast table. George entered the room, but instead of taking his accustomed seat near the door by which he had entered, passed it and went obliquely toward one of the windows—with what purpose no one knows. He had passed the table but a few steps when he fell heavily to the floor and did not again breathe. The body was carried into a bedroom and, after vain efforts at resuscitation by the stricken family, left lying on the bed with composed limbs and covered face.

===Chinese===
1872年8月14日早晨，住在俄亥俄州谢尼亚（Xenia, O.）的二十一岁青年乔治·J·里德（George J. Reid）在穿过父亲家的餐厅时摔倒了。家人包括他的父亲、母亲、两个姐妹和一个十五岁的表弟。所有人都在早餐桌旁。乔治走进房间，但他没有像往常一样在进门的门边坐下，而是走过座位，斜着走向一扇窗户——目的无人知晓。他刚走过桌子几步，就重重地摔倒在地板上，再也没有呼吸。尸体被抬进一间卧室，在悲痛的家人徒劳地尝试复苏后，被留在床上，四肢放平，脸被盖住。

===Original===
In the meantime the boy had been hastily dispatched for a physician, who arrived some twenty minutes after the death. He afterward remembered as an uncommon circumstance that when he arrived the weeping relations—father, mother, and two sisters—were all in the room out of which the bedroom door opened, and that the door was closed. There was no other door to the bedroom. This door was at once opened by the father of the deceased, and as the physician passed through it he observed the dead man’s clothing lying in a heap on the floor. He saw, too, the outlines of the body under the sheet that had been thrown over it; and the profile was plainly discernible under the face-cloth, clear-cut and sharp, as profiles of the dead seem always to be. He approached and lifted the cloth. There was nothing there. He pulled away the sheet. Nothing.

===Chinese===
与此同时，那个男孩被匆忙派去找医生，医生在死后大约二十分钟到达。他后来记得一个不寻常的情况：当他到达时，哭泣的亲属——父亲、母亲和两个姐妹——都在卧室门外的那个房间里，而且门是关着的。卧室没有别的门。死者的父亲立刻打开了这扇门，当医生走进去时，他看到死者的衣服堆在地板上。他还看到了覆盖在尸体上的床单下身体的轮廓；面巾下清晰可辨的侧脸，轮廓分明而清晰，就像死者的侧脸总是显得那样。他走上前去，掀开了面巾。那里什么都没有。他拉开了床单。什么都没有。

===Original===
The family had followed him into the room. At this astonishing discovery—if so it may be called—they looked at one another, at the physician, at the bed, in speechless amazement, forgetting to weep. A moment later the three ladies required the physician’s care. The father’s condition was but little better; he stood in a stupor, muttering inarticulately and staring like an idiot.

===Chinese===
家人跟着他进了房间。面对这惊人的发现——如果可以这样称呼的话——他们面面相觑，看看医生，看看床，惊讶得说不出话来，忘记了哭泣。片刻之后，三位女士需要医生的照料。父亲的情况也好不到哪里去；他呆立着，含糊不清地咕哝着，像白痴一样瞪着眼。

===Original===
Having restored the ladies to a sense of their surroundings, the physician went to the window—the only one the room had, opening upon a garden. It was locked on the inside with the usual fastening attached to the bottom bar of the upper sash and engaging with the lower.

===Chinese===
使女士们恢复了对周围环境的感知后，医生走向窗户——这是房间里唯一的窗户，对着一个花园。它从里面锁着，是常见的那种固定在上窗格底栏、与下窗格相连的插销。

===Original===
No inquest was held—there was nothing to hold it on; but the physician and many others who were curious as to this occurrence made the most searching investigation into all the circumstances; all without result. George Reid was dead and “gone,” and that is all that is known to this day.

===Chinese===
没有进行验尸——因为无尸可验；但医生和许多对这件事好奇的人对所有情况进行了最彻底的调查；结果一无所获。乔治·里德死了并“消失”了，这就是直到今天所知道的一切。

### A Cold Night / 寒夜

===Original===
The first day's battle at Stone River had been fought, resulting in disaster to the Federal army, which had been driven from its original ground at every point except its extreme left. The weary troops at this point lay behind a railway embankment to which they had retired, and which had served them during the last hours of the fight as a breastwork to repel repeated charges of the enemy. Behind the line the ground was open and rocky. Great boulders lay about everywhere, and among them lay many of the Federal dead, where they had been carried out of the way. Before the embankment the dead of both armies lay more thickly, but they had not been disturbed.

===Chinese===
斯通河（Stone River）战役的第一天已经打完，结果对联邦军（Federal）而言是一场灾难，除了最左翼外，他们已被从所有阵地上击退。疲惫不堪的部队撤退到一处铁路路基后方，在战斗的最后几个小时里，这里一直被用作抵御敌人反复冲锋的胸墙。防线后方是开阔而多石的地面。巨大的岩石到处散落，许多联邦军的阵亡者就躺在其中，被抬到那里以免碍事。路基前方，两军的死者躺得更为密集，但尚未被触动。

===Original===
Among the dead in the boulders lay one whom nobody seemed to know—a Federal sergeant, shot directly in the center of the forehead. One of our surgeons, from idle curiosity, or possibly with a view to the amusement of a group of officers during a lull in the engagement (we needed some thing to divert our minds), had pushed his probe clean through the head. The body lay on its back, its chin in the air, and with straightened limbs, as rigid as steel; frost on its white face and in its beard and hair. Some Christian soul had covered it with a blanket, but when the night became pretty sharp a companion of the writer removed this, and we lay beneath it our selves.

===Chinese===
在岩石丛中的死者里，躺着一个似乎无人认识的人——一名联邦军中士，额头正中央被子弹击中。我们的一位军医，出于无聊的好奇心，或者可能是为了在战斗间隙逗乐一群军官（我们需要点东西转移注意力），将他的探针完全穿过了头部。尸体仰面躺着，下巴朝天，四肢伸直，僵硬如钢铁；霜落在他苍白的脸上，以及他的胡须和头发里。某个好心人曾用一条毯子盖住他，但当夜晚变得相当寒冷时，笔者的一位同伴掀掉了毯子，我们自己躺在了它下面。

===Original===
With the exception of our pickets, who had been posted well out in front of the embankment, every man lay silent. Conversation was forbidden; to have made a fire, or even struck a match to light a pipe would have been a grave offense. Stamping horses, moaning wounded—everything that made a noise had been sent to the rear; the silence was absolute. Those whom the chill prevented from sleeping nevertheless reclined as they shivered, or sat with their hands on their arms, suffering but making no sign. Everyone had lost friends, and all expected death on the morrow. These matters are mentioned to show the improbability of anyone going about during those solemn hours to commit a ghastly practical joke.

===Chinese===
除了部署在路基前方远处的哨兵外，每个人都沉默地躺着。禁止交谈；生火，甚至划根火柴点烟斗，都会是严重的过失。踩踏的马匹、呻吟的伤员——所有会发出声响的东西都被送到了后方；寂静是绝对的。那些因寒冷而无法入睡的人仍然躺着发抖，或双手抱着膝盖坐着，默默忍受着痛苦。每个人都失去了朋友，所有人都预期明天会死。提到这些是为了表明，在那些肃穆的时刻，不可能有人四处走动去开一个可怕的玩笑。

===Original===
When the dawn broke the sky was still clear. "We shall have a warm day," the writer's companion whispered as we rose in the gray light; "let's give back the poor devil his blanket."

===Chinese===
破晓时分，天空依然晴朗。“今天会是个暖和天，”笔者的同伴在灰蒙蒙的晨光中起身时低语道；“让我们把毯子还给那可怜的家伙吧。”

===Original===
The sergeant's body lay in the same place, two yards away. But not in the same attitude. It was upon its right side. The knees were drawn up nearly to the breast, both hands thrust to the wrist between the buttons of the jacket, the collar of which was turned up, concealing the ears. The shoulders were elevated, the head was retracted, the chin rested on the collar bone. The posture was that of one suffering from intense cold. But for what had been previously observed—but for the ghastly evidence of the bullet-hole—one might have thought the man had died of cold.

===Chinese===
中士的尸体躺在原处，两码开外。但姿势不同了。它侧卧着，右侧朝下。膝盖几乎蜷缩到胸前，双手手腕以下都插在外套的纽扣之间，衣领竖起，遮住了耳朵。肩膀耸起，头向后缩，下巴搁在锁骨上。这是一个遭受严寒折磨的人的姿势。若非先前观察到的情况——若非弹孔那骇人的证据——人们可能会以为这个人是冻死的。

## A Creature of Habit / 习惯的产物

===Original===
At Hawley’s Bar, a mining camp near Virginia City, Mont., a gambler named Henry Graham, but commonly known as “Gray Hank,” met a miner named Dreyfuss one day, with whom he had had a dispute the previous night about a game of cards, and asked him into a barroom to have a drink. The unfortunate miner, taking this as an overture of peace, gladly accepted. They stood at the counter, and while Dreyfuss was in the act of drinking Graham shot him dead. This was in 1865. Within an hour after the murder Graham was in the hands of the vigilantes, and that evening at sunset, after a fair, if informal, trial, he was hanged to the limb of a tree which grew upon a little eminence within sight of the whole camp. The original intention had been to “string him up,” as is customary in such affairs; and with a view to that operation the long rope had been thrown over the limb, while a dozen pairs of hands were ready to hoist away. For some reason this plan was abandoned; the free end of the rope was made fast to a bush and the victim compelled to stand on the back of a horse, which at the cut of a whip sprang from under him, leaving him swinging. When steadied, his feet were about eighteen inches from the earth.

===Chinese===
在蒙大拿州弗吉尼亚城附近的一个采矿营地霍利酒吧（Hawley's Bar），一个名叫亨利·格雷厄姆（Henry Graham）的赌徒，通常被称为“灰汉克（Gray Hank）”，有一天遇到了一个名叫德雷福斯（Dreyfuss）的矿工，他前一晚因一局纸牌游戏与这人发生过争执，便请他到酒吧间喝一杯。这位不幸的矿工把这当作和平的提议，欣然接受了。他们站在柜台边，当德雷福斯正在喝酒时，格雷厄姆开枪打死了他。这是在1865年。谋杀发生后不到一个小时，格雷厄姆就落入了治安维持会的手中，那天傍晚日落时分，在一次公正但非正式的审判后，他被吊在了一棵长在小山丘上的树枝上，整个营地都能看见。最初的意图是按这类事件的惯例“把他吊起来”；为了这个操作，长绳已经抛过了树枝，十几双手正准备拉起。但出于某种原因，这个计划被放弃了；绳子的自由端被拴在了一棵灌木上，受害者被迫站在一匹马的背上，马在鞭子的抽打下从他身下跳开，留他在空中摇晃。等稳定下来时，他的脚离地面大约十八英寸。

===Original===
The body remained suspended for exactly half an hour, the greater part of the crowd remaining about it: then the “judge” ordered it taken down. The rope was untied from the bush, and two men stood by to lower away. The moment the feet came squarely upon the ground the men engaged in lowering, thinking doubtless that those standing about the body had hold of it to support it, let go the rope. The body at once ran quickly forward toward the main part of the crowd, the rope paying out as it went. The head rolled from side to side, the eyes and tongue protruding, the face ghastly purple, the lips covered with bloody froth. With cries of horror the crowd ran hither and thither, stumbling, falling over one another, cursing. In and out among them—over the fallen, coming into collision with others, the horrible dead man “pranced,” his feet lifted so high at each step that his knees struck his breast, his tongue swinging like that of a panting dog, the foam flying in flakes from his swollen lips. The deepening twilight added its terror to the scene, and men fled from the spot, not daring to look behind.
Straight into this confusion from the outskirts of the crowd walked with rapid steps the tall figure of a man whom all who saw instantly recognized as a master spirit. This was Dr. Arnold Spier, who with two other physicians had pronounced the man dead and had been retiring to the camp. He moved as directly toward the dead man as the now somewhat less rapid and erratic movements of the latter would permit, and seized him in his arms. Encouraged by this, a score of men sprang shouting to the free end of the rope, which had not been drawn entirely over the limb, and laid hold of it, intending to make a finish of their work. They ran with it toward the bush to which it had been fastened, but there was no resistance; the physician had cut it from the murderer’s neck. In a moment the body was lying on its back, with composed limbs and face up turned to the kindling stars, in the motionless rigidity appropriate to death. The hanging had been done well enough—the neck was broken.
“The dead are creatures of habit,” said Dr. Spier. “A corpse which when on its feet will walk and run will lie still when placed on its back.”

===Chinese===
尸体悬挂了正好半个小时，大部分人群仍留在周围：然后“法官”命令把它放下来。绳子从灌木上解开，两个人站在旁边准备放下。当脚一接触地面，负责放绳的人以为站在尸体周围的人扶住了尸体，就松开了绳子。尸体立刻快速向前冲向人群的主体，绳子随之放出去。头颅左右摇晃，眼睛和舌头凸出，脸是可怕的紫色，嘴唇上沾满了血沫。随着恐怖的叫声，人群四散奔逃，跌跌撞撞，互相践踏，咒骂着。在这群人中间穿行——越过倒地的人，与其他人相撞——那个可怕的死人在“跳跃”，每一步都把脚抬得很高，膝盖撞到胸口，舌头像喘气的狗一样摆动，泡沫从他肿胀的嘴唇上成片地飞溅出来。渐深的暮色给这一场景增添了恐怖，人们逃离了那个地方，不敢回头看。
一个高大的身影从人群外围快步走进这片混乱之中，所有看到的人立刻认出他是一位领袖人物。这是阿诺德·斯皮尔（Arnold Spier）医生，他和另外两位医生曾宣告此人死亡，当时正要返回营地。他尽可能直接地朝那个死人走去（此时后者的动作已不那么急促和不规则了），一把将他抱在怀里。受到鼓励，二十几个人喊叫着冲向绳子的自由端——那绳子并未完全拉过树枝——抓住了它，打算完成他们的工作。他们拉着绳子向拴绳子的灌木跑去，但没有任何抵抗；医生已经把绳子从凶手的脖子上割断了。片刻之间，尸体平躺在地上，四肢安放整齐，脸朝向闪烁的星星，呈现出与死亡相称的静止僵硬。绞刑执行得相当成功——脖子已经折断了。
“死者是习惯的产物，”斯皮尔医生说。“一具尸体，当它站着时会行走和奔跑，但当它平躺时就会静止不动。”

### The Difficulty of Crossing a Field / 穿越田野之难

===Original===
One morning in July, 1854, a planter named Williamson, living six miles from Selma, Alabama, was sitting with his wife and a child on the veranda of his dwelling. Immediately in front of the house was a lawn, perhaps fifty yards in extent between the house and public road, or, as it was called, the "pike." Beyond this road lay a close-cropped pasture of some ten acres, level and without a tree, rock, or any natural or artificial object on its surface. At the time there was not even a domestic animal in the field. In another field, beyond the pasture, a dozen slaves were at work under an overseer.

===Chinese===
1854年7月的一个早晨，一位名叫威廉森（Williamson）的种植园主，住在阿拉巴马州塞尔玛（Selma, Alabama）六英里外，正与妻子和一个孩子坐在自家门廊上。房子正前方是一片草坪，从房子到公共道路——或者像人们所说的那样，叫"大路"（pike）——之间约有五十码的距离。这条路那边是一片约十英亩的牧场，地势平坦，没有树木、岩石或任何天然或人工的物体。当时田里甚至没有一头牲畜。在牧场那边的另一块地里，十几个奴隶正在监工的监督下干活。

===Original===
Throwing away the stump of a cigar, the planter rose, saying: "I forgot to tell Andrew about those horses." Andrew was the overseer.

===Chinese===
种植园主扔掉雪茄烟头，站起身说："我忘了告诉安德鲁（Andrew）那几匹马的事。"安德鲁是监工。

===Original===
Williamson strolled leisurely down the gravel walk, plucking a flower as he went, passed across the road and into the pasture, pausing a moment as he closed the gate leading into it, to greet a passing neighbour, Armour Wren, who lived on an adjoining plantation. Mr. Wren was in an open carriage with his son James, a lad of thirteen. When he had driven some two hundred yards from the point of meeting, Mr. Wren said to his son: "I forgot to tell Mr. Williamson about those horses."

===Chinese===
威廉森悠闲地沿着碎石小路漫步，边走边摘了一朵花，穿过大路走进牧场，在关上通往牧场的大门时停了一下，向路过的一位邻居阿莫尔·雷恩（Armour Wren）打招呼，雷恩住在邻近的一个种植园里。雷恩先生坐在一辆敞篷马车里，和他十三岁的儿子詹姆斯（James）在一起。当马车驶离相遇点大约两百码时，雷恩先生对他儿子说："我忘了告诉威廉森先生那几匹马的事。"

===Original===
Mr. Wren had sold to Mr. Williamson some horses, which were to have been sent for that day, but for some reason not now remembered it would be inconvenient to deliver them until the morrow. The coachman was directed to drive back, and as the vehicle turned Williamson was seen by all three, walking leisurely across the pasture. At that moment one of the coach horses stumbled and came near falling. It had no more than fairly recovered itself when James Wren cried: "Why, father, what has become of Mr. Williamson?"

===Chinese===
雷恩先生曾卖给威廉森先生几匹马，原定那天来取，但出于某种现在记不清的原因，要到明天才能送来。车夫被吩咐掉头回去，当马车转弯时，他们三人都看到威廉森正悠闲地穿过牧场。就在这时，拉车的一匹马绊了一下，差点摔倒。它刚站稳脚跟，詹姆斯·雷恩就叫道："咦，爸爸，威廉森先生哪儿去了？"

===Original===
It is not the purpose of this narrative to answer that question.

===Chinese===
本文无意回答这个问题。

===Original===
Mr. Wren's strange account of the matter, given under oath in the course of legal proceedings relating to the Williamson estate, here follows:

===Chinese===
以下是雷恩先生在有关威廉森遗产的法律程序中宣誓后对这件事的奇特陈述：

===Original===
"My son's exclamation caused me to look toward the spot where I had seen the deceased [*sic*] an instant before, but he was not there, nor was he anywhere visible. I cannot say that at the moment I was greatly startled, or realized the gravity of the occurrence, though I thought it singular. My son, however, was greatly astonished and kept repeating his question in different forms until we arrived at the gate. My black boy Sam was similarly affected, even in a greater degree, but I reckon more by my son's manner than by anything he had himself observed. [This sentence in the testimony was stricken out.] As we got out of the carriage at the gate of the field, and while Sam was hanging [*sic*] the team to the fence, Mrs. Williamson, with her child in her arms and followed by several servants, came running down the walk in great excitement, crying: 'He is gone, he is gone! O God! what an awful thing!' and many other such exclamations, which I do not distinctly recollect. I got from them the impression that they related to something more than the mere disappearance of her husband, even if that had occurred before her eyes. Her manner was wild, but not more so, I think, than was natural under the circumstances. I have no reason to think she had at that time lost her mind. I have never since seen nor heard of Mr. Williamson."

===Chinese===
"我儿子的惊叫使我朝我刚才看到死者（原文如此）的地方望去，但他不在那里，任何地方都看不见。我不能说那一刻我有多震惊，或者意识到这件事的严重性，虽然我觉得很奇怪。但我儿子却非常惊讶，一直用不同方式重复他的问题，直到我们到达大门口。我的黑人男孩萨姆（Sam）也同样受到影响，甚至更严重，但我猜想更多是被我儿子的态度所影响，而不是他自己观察到了什么。[证词中的这句话被删掉了。]当我们在牧场门口下车，萨姆正在（原文如此）把马拴到篱笆上时，威廉森夫人抱着孩子，在几个仆人的跟随下，非常激动地沿着小路跑来，哭喊着：'他不见了，他不见了！上帝啊！多么可怕的事啊！'以及许多其他类似的惊呼，我记不清了。我从他们那里得到的印象是，这些话所指的不仅仅是她丈夫的失踪，即使那是在她眼前发生的。她的举止狂乱，但我想，在那种情况下，这也很自然。我没有理由认为她那时已经疯了。我此后再也没有见过或听说过威廉森先生。"

===Original===
